# Stdio Goal Execution

## Scope And Current Implementation

The general Stdio Rust package now accepts a caller-authored ContributionRegistry through AssemblyPlan/AssemblyOwner and an existing typed PluginBuilder. It has no concrete artifact dependencies, family dev dependencies, selected roster, component metadata/world, compatibility catalog facade or default artifact features. Native factory receipts remain schema-authorized and must match the selected runtime declarations bijectively. Atomic registration/removal and schema/identity/MIME/extension uniqueness validation remain in the general registry.

The 36-artifact deployment roster, 18-app runtime dispatch, concrete catalog/receipt projection, schemas, fixtures, native tests and Rust command implementation moved to `🌎️hub/🧩️compositions/🗄️stdio`; the actual component identity remains `semio:stdio`, the Cargo owner is `semio-hub-stdio` with semantic role hub/component-kind plugin. Native receipt protocol paths point to their workspace-contained artifact owners. Hub consumers and command references were updated; root owns neutral publication/discovery integration. GIS/VCS, nine Stdio family deployments and the remaining 31 concrete native assemblies are physically owned by Hub, with actual identities, app rosters and native codec bindings retained. The Jack-specific CLI belongs to the Jack artifact; shared Norm constructors and GIS Map law inputs belong to their lower domain contracts/artifacts.

EPW owns its MIME policy as a JSON schema constraint. General contribution validation consumes an optional authored constraint without an EPW MIME branch. Neutral alpha vectors independently prove custom MIME admission and authored MIME refusal. EPW callback/schema tests preserve MIME absence.

ArtifactPack declares an optional relational snapshot codec, absent by default. Actual SQLite owners explicitly publish their existing handwritten relational capability. ArtifactCodec::bare admits pack-only snapshots; the existing required ArtifactCodec::of constructor remains SQLite-constrained. General app/editor/viewer bounds and registration plans no longer require relational capability where only native pack/text behavior is invoked. Native document codec publication always retains pack behavior and only publishes actual relational snapshot registrations; explicit SQLite registration stays strict. EPW gains no synthetic persistence implementation.

## Verification And Remaining Checks

- Test-first generic contract portable check was RED on the old EPW branch.
- General assembly canonical-architecture registered Nx route is GREEN10 independent AJV/Bun/Iarna checks.
- Actual general assembly registered Cargo check (--offline) is GREEN, finished in1m45s after shared framework compilation.
- General contribution canonical-architecture registered Nx route is GREEN25 independent AJV/schema/static checks.
- EPW portable twin is GREEN2 and actual registered native definition/pack-only codec laws are GREEN2 (4m01s), with runtime evidence.
- Both new/general and moved/concrete permanent scripts successfully compile with Bun's bundler syntax check. This does not claim runtime behavior.
- Fresh deletion-proof command constructs a regular-file Cargo workspace without concrete Stdio artifacts or Hub composition, checks the real general package, and then checks compiler refusal of a pack-only generic caller at the explicit SQLite constructor. Registered actual execution is GREEN2m43s: concrete artifactsAbsent=true, all36 first-party closure owners and11,427 regular copied sourcefiles, fresh Cargo check2m04s, and exactlyone E0277 compiler refusal P:ArtifactSqliteSnapshot. Runtime DEBUG evidence confirms both outcomes. Earlier proof attempts omitted the actual paired nx.json/📋project.json owner markers; MutationLeaf correctly rejected their provenance. The permanent proof now copies those regular owner inputs and retains strict source authority.
- Actual native general assembly is GREEN1 / five authored removal vectors (2m32s); neutral optional relational capability is GREEN1 / two cases (36s); neutral owned contribution constraint is GREEN1 / four cases (48.8s). Temporary runtime DEBUG statements were removed after recording results. GLTF policy native verification is GREEN1 / six cases plus seven portable checks. Final fresh deletion proof is GREEN for real general Stdio, generic 3D and selected Session with all concrete Stdio artifacts absent: 52 owners / 11,555 files in the final integrated rerun. Concrete Stdio/GIS/VCS deployment/provider routes, 31-component native laws and cleanup remain pending.
- Actual concrete registered Stdio/GIS/VCS routes retain their required inferred dependencies. Attempts caught current source integration failures in exact generator/browser export authority and concurrent generic spatial extraction; executors corrected the bindings and spatial source is being verified. No exact-import authority was relaxed.

### Current Removal Proof And Concrete Integration

The fresh regular-file removal proof completed GREEN after the spatial extraction settled: general Stdio and generic 3D compiled with concrete Stdio artifacts absent (36 owners / 11,530 files, 2m54s). A second actual run explicitly selected neutral `semio-s-spatial-kernel-semio-session` via `deletion-proof --owner`, followed its authored Cargo closure and compiled all three general crates with the same absent artifact tree: 52 owners / 11,551 regular files, 2m29s. These prove the selected general closure is independent of deleted Stdio artifacts; they do not prove every remaining closed concrete plugin catalog is removable.

Scoped portable GIS and VCS codec oracles passed (4.0s and 2.0s). The seed owner subsequently confirmed settled source; all three full production routes are rerunning. GIS/VCS artifact-output configuration now resolves the explicit ticket environment against repo root before execution, ensuring native receipts stay ticket-local.

The outward Stdio editor source census now matches the current authored macro rows with two test names. Its SQLite editor law consults the actual optional relational capability: bare snapshots prove absence of a relational route; declared SQLite snapshots retain their actual SQL export/import parity laws. No synthetic EPW SQLite implementation was added.
